# Gate-eligible smoke register (run by quality_gates/run_smokes.py).
#
# One repo-relative *_smoke.py path per line. A smoke gates the commit only
# once it is listed here. This is a tracked, growing allowlist (same model as
# god_class_allowlist.txt / radon_*_allowlist.txt): adding a smoke is an
# explicit, reviewable act, and the gate never silently broadens to smokes that
# need a live platform. Every entry MUST pass offline (no live solet / LM Studio),
# with one ratified exception class (GTE-09, Coordinator-Day Q2): a smoke whose
# LOAD-BEARING proof genuinely requires a live external dependency (e.g.
# external_postgres_plugin's smoke_readonly.py/smoke_multistatement.py, a real
# Postgres) may register if an unreachable dependency is a VISIBLE, disclosed
# outcome — an unmissable banner plus the dedicated SKIP exit code (77, the
# automake/Meson/CTest convention; UPDATED 2026-08-08, undeclared-dependency
# audit: run_smokes.py now reports passed/skipped/failed distinctly, so the
# GTE-09-era fail-loud-instead-of-silent-skip workaround is no longer needed) —
# rather than a silent skip. The offline rule is about invisible scope creep,
# not about barring a documented, disclosed live-dependency smoke.
#
# Seed set = the blue->green TRANSITION-critical smokes (the cutover must not be
# confirmed with any of these red). Grow as new gate-relevant smokes land
# (e.g. the proposed S1 post-transition-invariant + S2 readiness-truth smokes).

# Canonical macOS business-operations product identity: validates the exact
# repository/bundle/profile/runtime carrier and line-classifies every retained
# former token; unmatched or stale live identities fail closed. Offline.

quality_gates/tests/allowlist_tagging_smoke.py

# files only.
quality_gates/tests/allowlist_schema_smoke.py

# registered here or appear in the tagged exact-path exclusions register.

# the SAME role is a no-op refresh (branch i); a second NAMED role while the
# first binding still names this session is refused CardinalityConflictError
# (branch ii), never silently allowed; a stale orphan self-repairs in place
# (branch iii); a lost predicate on the self-repair re-enters the bounded
# loop rather than falling through (Architect ratification #1); the
# displacer's cleanup delete is predicated (fires / benign no-op). TRUST-MODEL
# HONEST: genuine against an honestly-identified session, NOT hardened

# guard fired each time: (a) the displacer's cleanup delete disabled ->
# displacement still succeeds, the loser's row persists, and its OWN next
# claim self-repairs via branch (iii); (b) a clean displacement -> the
# loser's row is gone immediately (the delete fired, branch iii never ran).

# Fleet session-management Phase B, D1 §5 — the 'headless' HostAdapter

# (AGENT_SESSION_ID minted exactly once, label falls back to agent_instance_id
# when no lane_id) via a records-only fake popen_fn; alive()/terminate()/
# shutdown() against REAL short-lived subprocesses (real kill -0 + SIGTERM/
# SIGKILL + reap, never the real claude binary); driver_channel() returns None
# for any host_ref this adapter's in-memory map doesn't recognize (the exact
# post-restart case clear_session/compact_session will check, AMEND 5b) OR
# whose tracked pid has since died on its own without ever being terminate()'d
# — a zombie still answers kill(pid, 0) until reaped, so alive()/
# driver_channel()/_sigterm_then_kill all use Popen.poll() (which reaps) for
# any TRACKED process rather than the raw kill-0 probe (only used for an
# untracked host_ref, e.g. post-restart); send() on a dead process's stdin
# swallows the broken pipe (fire-and-forget contract) instead of raising an
# unmapped exception through the clear_session/compact_session verb layer.

# directly: a bare None filter value matches ZERO rows (never a NULL cell —
# mirrors the real postgres provider's `col = NULL`, always false, NOT

# repo_service SEARCH FALLBACK (2026-08-08, seed-adoption) — `search` hard-failed
# without ripgrep, which nothing in the adopter install path installs or even
# mentions. It now falls back to the git-native grep, which genesis GUARANTEES
# because it git-inits every born tree, so the fallback cannot itself become the
# undeclared dependency it replaces. Pins: the fallback runs with rg absent; it
# returns the SAME hits as ripgrep for a literal query; it uses PCRE and NOT POSIX
# ERE (measured — ERE agrees with ripgrep on 0% of \d / \w queries, returning WRONG
# hits rather than fewer, so a silent downgrade would trade a loud failure for a
# quiet wrong answer); and a malformed pattern still raises instead of collapsing
# into "no hits".

# refresh_plugin_process result-contract collision (result-processing lane (a),
# 2026-08-01). The verb's return schema named its top-level field `process_key`
# for the key it had just REFRESHED, while the result contract reserves that
# name for the key of the verb that PRODUCED the result — so
# _check_result_process_key_matches raised RESULT_CONTRACT_VIOLATION on EVERY
# call, after the side-effect had landed and a completed row had been stored.
# Callers saw success; the parent flow token was blocked. Renamed to
# `refreshed_process_key`. Drives the REAL validate_common_success path and
# DERIVES its payload from the code (decorator metadata + ast over the return
# site), so it tracks the verb instead of a hand-typed dict. Mutation table
# M1-M5 in workbench/2026-08-01_refresh_verb_mutation_harness_claude_a.py:
# 5/5 red, incl. M4 which was BLIND until the envelope was bound to
# execute_action's source too.

# load-bearing plist field: Label=local.solet.<name>;
# EnvironmentVariables sets SOLET_NAME (boot fast-fails without it);
# WorkingDirectory is home_dir/.ananta/runtime/<name> — deliberately NOT
# ananta.core.runtime.get_runtime_dir() (verified 2026-07-09: that helper
# returns one SHARED ~/.ananta/runtime dir with no per-name segment despite
# accepting a solet_name param); ProgramArguments launches ananta.cli
# directly (no supervisor) via the clone's own absolute .venv/bin/python3 +
# absolute --app-home profile dir (never a bare "profile" string, which
# would resolve against the runtime dir instead); RunAtLoad=true;
# KeepAlive is the SIMPLE crash-restart form ({SuccessfulExit: false}), not
# an unconditional <true/>. Also pins install/uninstall/status idempotency
# (unload-before-reload on a stale plist, absent-uninstall is a no-op
# success, a launchctl failure raises AutostartError). Offline (tmpfs

# — no real /bin/launchctl, filesystem writes outside tmp, or
# ~/Library/LaunchAgents touched).
#
# Codex F must-fix (2026-07-09, folded post-review): the launchctl-list
# PROBE no longer coerces "execution failed" into "not loaded" — only the
# documented not-found signal (stderr contains "Could not find service")
# maps to False; an execution failure (OSError/timeout) or any other
# unrecognized nonzero-exit shape raises LaunchctlObservationError instead
# of guessing. Pins: (a) status() raises/surfaces the failure rather than
# reporting a fabricated not_installed; (b) uninstall() REFUSES to act on
# an unobservable state — no unload attempt, plist left in place, so a
# transient list failure can never orphan a live launchd job with no
# backing plist; (c) the discrimination boundary itself — an unrecognized
# nonzero exit is not silently re-widened back into "not loaded".

# github_midwife_plugin coordination-hooks SECURITY-CLAIM suite (2026-07-29,
# Coordinator-Day; git_controller_gate_smoke.py inverted in 2026-07-30,
# Git-Controller) — the plugin goes to corporate security review as a
# standalone directory, and SECURITY.md is the document that IS that review. It
# makes ~10 falsifiable claims and, before this suite, nothing verified any of
# them: the word "test" did not appear in it. The drift is not hypothetical —
# adding a fifth hook falsified its "fixed string literal" claim and its hook
# counts (fixed by hand in 92f41b774), and plugin.json's description had
# silently omitted wake_waiter.js before that.
#
# UNLIKE every other entry here, these four live INSIDE the plugin
# (claude_plugin/coordination-hooks/tests/) rather than in the plugin's tests/
# dir, because the reviewed artifact must carry its own evidence: `cd
# coordination-hooks && python3 tests/run_all.py` is verified green on macOS's
# own python3 3.9.6, under `env -i`, outside any git repo, creating no file
# inside the artifact. Nothing in them resolves a path outside the plugin, and
# manifest_consistency_smoke.py asserts that so it cannot regress. `**/tests/`
# is a categorical seed prune, so seeds still ship the plugin without them —
# reviewed is a superset of shipped, which is the safe direction.
#
# No pytest and no npm: a test framework inside the artifact would contradict
# the stdlib-only supply-chain claim these very tests defend. The harness is
# stdlib Python driving the Node hooks as subprocesses — the same contract
# Claude Code uses, and the only contract the Node hooks have (they export
# nothing). Child environments are built explicitly, never inherited, so a
# "disarmed" case cannot silently run against a hook the launcher already armed.
#
# Every check was mutation-verified: 17 deliberate defects (relayed child
# output, propagated nonzero exit, raw-label concatenation, inline shell in the
# manifest, added network import, second subprocess, third-party require,
# count drift, undocumented sixth hook, ...) each turn the relevant smoke red.
# That pass found a real hole — capturing the child's streams via `stdio: pipe`
# falsifies "dropped unread" while leaking nothing observable, so the "unread"
# claim needed a source-level check, not a black-box one. Offline (tmpfs only).

# T1 usage-capture lane (2026-08-05) — .claude/hooks/capture_session_mapping.py,
# the SessionStart hook that self-declares the agent_instance_id <->
# claude_session_id mapping to a file-per-firing spool
# (ANANTA_SESSION_MAPPING_SPOOL_DIR, declared by both host adapters, never
# derived here). Non-fatal by design (ruling Q1(e)): missing env vars, no
# session_id in the payload, malformed stdin JSON, and an unwritable spool
# path all exit 0 with a stderr warning, never disrupting the worker's
# session start. Covers the happy path (one correctly-shaped, correctly-
# named spool file per firing), the capture_source composite token
# (hook:<source>, falling back to hook:unknown when source is absent), and
# every non-fatal failure leg.

# T2 (fleet-token-efficiency lane, 2026-08-05) — .claude/hooks/heartbeat_report_alive.py,
# the PostToolUse heartbeat that replaced a REJECTED detached-shell-loop design
# (seat's redesign ruling, after pid-32482 was measured live surviving a fleet
# /clear and stamping an effectively-dead session's row for ~10 hours). This
# hook has NO persistent process of its own -- it only runs synchronously as
# part of an actual tool-call lifecycle, so it dies with the context it
# vouches for BY CONSTRUCTION. Throttled to at most once per _THROTTLE_SECONDS
# via a per-worker local marker file's mtime. Covers: missing env vars are
# non-fatal; a fresh marker throttles the very next firing; a stale marker
# allows another call; a FAILED report_alive call never touches the marker
# (so the next firing retries, never silently suppressed); many rapid firings
# under one identity still throttle correctly throughout (e.g. across a
# /clear -- the worker is genuinely still alive, continued heartbeating is
# correct there); and the RED-FIRST structural false-liveness guard --
# inspects the hook's own source for ANY backgrounding primitive (Popen,
# os.fork, nohup, disown, start_new_session) and asserts none exist.

# snowflake_plugin — Wave-2 Lane A, the read-only Snowflake warehouse connector
# (SNW-A..E, 2026-07-13, built from the ratified-but-never-built umbrella design's
# wave-2 slot; D0.3 async containment added 2026-08-10, lane W2c). FIVE hermetic
# smokes: smoke_config (snowflake_account resolve incl. a REAL RSA-2048 PEM
# round-trip through the resolver + eager fail-loud parse of a flattened key +
# repr redaction + statement/login timeout bounds), smoke_guard (the read-leader
# belt — FAST-FAIL ONLY, no session-level read-only flag exists for Snowflake
# unlike Postgres, so the guard cannot itself stop a write; the true boundary is
# the operator-granted read-only role, documented not exercised here), smoke_query
# (read verbs + TOPOLOGY-LEAK red-first at classify level + EDGE-parity; the
# plugin-level dispatch->driver-fault integration now runs entirely inside the
# D0.3 background worker, covered by smoke_async_jobs.py below, not duplicated
# here), smoke_data_export (run_query fail-loud overflow red-first + export_query
# workspace-TSV write via the REAL containment gate, same red-first family as the

# salesforce_plugin D0.3 deferred-completion migration (sync-verb Phase 1 wave
# 2, lane W2b, 2026-08-10 — mirrors external_postgres_plugin's landed batch 1
# shape above, the closest exemplar: single-round-trip-per-verb I/O via a
# driver/CLI executor object, not paginated). All 9 real-I/O verbs
# (soql_query/export_soql/get_record/describe_sobject/list_sobjects/
# create_record/update_record/delete_record/test_connection) now dispatch
# ms-scale (job_id + status: queued, no SalesforceCliExecutor call on the
# dispatch path — proven directly) and complete on the ONE lazily-started
# background worker thread. smoke_async_jobs proves: (1) dispatch for all 9
# verbs returns queued without ever touching run_json/run_rest — the
# containment requirement this lane's brief named explicitly, (2) missing
# session_id/flow_id in state fails loud at dispatch, before any job is
# created, (3) the worker's _process_job runs the real record_actions/
# soql_actions function against a fake executor for a plain read
# (get_record), a write (create_record), the export-path-gated TSV verb
# (soql_query — the worker test proves the TSV actually gets written via
# plugin._export_path_gate, and the always-TSV contract survives the move
# off the dispatch path unchanged), and the special test_connection wrapper,
# completing the job with the correct result shape, (4) a REST-level fault
# classifies topology-safe through the SAME _run path smoke_records.py
# already covers, now reached from the worker instead of the dispatch
# handler, (5) ACTION_HANDLERS carries exactly the 9 migrated verb names — a
# typo here would silently strand that verb's queued jobs forever, (6)
# ensure_worker_started is idempotent (ONE worker thread, never more —
# FlowManager._sequence_cache's unlocked check-then-increment is unsafe for
# concurrent completions into the same flow).

# marketo_plugin — marketing-automation REST connector (2026-07-28), same
# auth-simplicity class as zuora_plugin (OAuth 2.0 client-credentials,
# chain-consumed client_secret) but with a materially different fault model:
# Marketo returns HTTP 200 with a JSON envelope (success: false + errors[])
# for almost every API-level fault, so MarketoClient decodes and classifies
# on the envelope, not the HTTP status — a 601/602 (invalid/expired token)
# envelope triggers exactly ONE re-mint-and-retry instead of a real 401.
# Unlike zuora_plugin, this connector DOES expose a delete verb
# (delete_leads) — Marketo natively supports lead deletion. Partial-batch
# results (create_or_update_leads/delete_leads/add_leads_to_list/
# remove_leads_from_list) stay success:true with per-record skipped/failed
# entries — not classified as an error. No async Bulk Extract job flow in v1
# (get_leads covers ad-hoc reads via the caller-driven Pattern-B loop, §7.2).
# Pure REST — no SQL-shaped strings anywhere, so the SQL-lockdown gate is
# silent for this plugin (verified: zero findings).
# Business-data limits + data-export migration (2026-08-02, §7.1): all six of
# describe_lead_fields/get_leads/list_activity_types/get_activities/
# list_campaigns/list_static_lists now ALWAYS write to a caller-supplied
# output_tsv_path — blob storage retired from this plugin entirely.
# Internal pagination is hidden by design (2026-08-03 hide-paging change,
# operator ruling "the paging is an implementation detail that should be
# hidden", design doc §5.4/§7.2 as amended): get_leads/get_activities/list_campaigns/list_static_lists now
# page INTERNALLY across Marketo's 300-per-call vendor ceiling up to a §5
# acknowledge_default_limit_override/row_limit-governed effective limit
# (default 500, hard cap 5,000) — no next_page_token/more_result field
# survives on any of the four; a truncated boolean replaces them. Beyond the
# hard cap: no resumption, re-invoke with a narrower filter/date-range.
# describe_lead_fields/list_activity_types are unchanged (single call, no
# pagination surface ever existed).
# FIVE hermetic smokes (httpx.MockTransport / faked client, no network):
# smoke_auth (instance config resolve, repr redaction, GET-based bearer-token
# caching-until-near-expiry, a 601 envelope triggering exactly ONE
# re-mint-and-retry, a failed token mint raising MarketoAuthError, EDGE
# parity), smoke_leads (describe_lead_fields/get_leads/get_activities/
# list_activity_types TSV-handle shape via a throwaway tempfile + passthrough
# gate, get_leads/get_activities internal-loop 2-page accumulation +
# hidden-field regression guard, get_activities' empty-mid-stream-page and
# tokenless-moreResult=true contract-violation loop behavior,
# create_or_update_leads/delete_leads batch validation + tallies proving
# partial-batch success is not an error, classify_marketo_envelope across the
# sourced 601/606/702/1005/unmapped code spread), smoke_campaigns_lists
# (list_campaigns/trigger_campaign/list_static_lists/add_leads_to_list
# validation, remove_leads_from_list proving the DELETE-with-JSON-body path
# uses delete_json, not post_json or httpx.Client.delete()),
# smoke_internal_paging (the named request-500-gets-2-internal-
# pages-one-complete-file acceptance scenario plus the shared §5
# override-friction 4-case set, per verb, for all four internally-paginated
# verbs), smoke_data_export (the REAL export_containment gate:
# outside-root/empty-roots/misconfigured-root red-first cases, an admitted
# path proceeding to the vendor call, and the regression guard that
# check_setup's permission probes need NO export_allowed_roots configured at
# all — a throwaway tempfile + passthrough gate, never the operator's real
# workspace).
plugins/marketo_plugin/tests/smoke_auth.py
plugins/marketo_plugin/tests/smoke_leads.py
plugins/marketo_plugin/tests/smoke_campaigns_lists.py
plugins/marketo_plugin/tests/smoke_internal_paging.py

# get_leads/get_activities/list_campaigns/list_static_lists dispatch handlers

# rather than running vendor I/O inline — dispatch-time session_id/flow_id

# never reach create_job; the background worker (_process_pending_jobs)

# trigger_poll dispatch-offload (2026-07-10 fleet-wide dispatch-wedge fix): the cron
# heartbeat trigger_poll now SUBMITS the whole importer pass to a single-slot
# BoundedSummaryExecutor (_poll_executor) and returns {"poller": started|already_running}
# in milliseconds — the 3rd instance of the reviewed heartbeat/drainer pattern — instead
# of running poll_once() INLINE on the action-queue thread (an inline ~1.2M-event first
# pass parked fleet-wide process_call dispatch: the serial _poll_once loop awaits each
# dispatch to completion). Pins (red-first): AST guard (trigger_poll delegates via
# start_importer_poll_drain with NO inline poll_once; the poll_once lives in
# run_importer_poll_drain), fast-return-while-blocked + off-thread execution, the
# singleton guard (second fire → already_running), and slot-release-on-exception (a

# (capabilities -> plugin set + template + baseline subset, O-3 named-bundle OR
# explicit-list) + its two data files (seed_manifest.yaml, capability_bundles.yaml).

# fail-loud when seed_factory_plugin is named, unknown/empty raises, data-file

# INF-06 reliability carve — forwarded-vertex holder-forward recoverability
# (2026-07-18). A died/timed-out sys:autonomic holder that was forwarded a
# Surface-1 action-decode vertex now re-drives instead of silent-stalling. The
# durable core__inference_deferred_vertex row gains a lifecycle STATE
# (deferred|forwarded|failed) + forwarded_at + a monotone attempts counter; the
# forward MINTS a 'forwarded' serve-anchor durability-FIRST (record_forwarded_vertex
# before provider.process_*; a flow_id-absent forward RAISES, a store failure
# propagates loud — never an anchorless fire-and-forget). The core half: the mint
# fail-loud contract, the sweep/drain read+write helpers (forwarded_before /
# live_rows_in_state / increment_attempts monotone / mark_terminal_failed /
# attempts_of), and the ★ Architect §6-bis freshness gate — a forwarded
# process_error turn does NOT advance the plan (should_skip_advancement guard,
# RED-FIRST control on process_results), so the failed step keeps its [>] current

# program's LAST slice: a NEW EDGE_SINK verb trigger_scheduled_self_vet fires the DAILY L1-only self-vet
# on a single-slot background executor (the slot IS the lease) and returns a fast started/already_running
# receipt — a full L1 self-vet is MINUTES so it must NOT run inline (action-queue fast-return contract).
# The background run goes through the PERSISTING L1 path (a vetting_runs row per run = the R9-3 trend
# baseline; substrate=heuristic) and queues a REGRESSION-ONLY memory note (tag
# code_vetting:self_vet_regression) when its blocker/high counts worsen vs the prior run (else silent —
# trend-not-nag). Pins: single-slot lease (in-flight → already_running; frees after the run); the verb
# FAST-RETURNS touching NO state/memory (the R6/boundary is smoke-visible via a raising service + a
# non-running executor); EDGE_SINK registration (processor_policy_category=edge_sink + NOT in
# get_edge_process_definitions, else edge_process_mismatch FATAL); the is_regression predicate (blocker
# OR high worsens → True; medium/low churn silent); the background body persists a row + queues the note
# only on a worse run. HERMETIC (fake create-before-upsert StateServiceProtocol + fake memory + a stub
# scan; no real scanners / live solet). Cron REGISTRATION is a post-deploy create_cron_schedule step (out
# of this slice). Design: workbench/2026-07-21_w3c3_trigger_scheduled_self_vet_design.md.

# Watcher lifecycle. Real subprocesses under a

# re-arm after a clean exit still succeeds, so the singleton is a live-holder

# rather than -15 by default disposition — the discriminator that proves the

# The dispatch liveness gate. `append_event` succeeds
# against ANY non-closed bridge, and a SIGKILLed watcher leaves its server-side
# session standing, so `queued_watcher` was reported for up to the full 3_600s
# idle sweep while nothing polled the queue. The SAFETY half is the priority
# half: every "stale is refused" case is paired with a "fresh still delivers"
# control, because an over-firing gate would stop delivery to healthy watchers —
# a worse regression than the defect. The knob is exercised at NON-DEFAULT
# values (300s and 30s) so a dropped config wiring cannot hide behind the 90s
# default.
plugins/agent_messaging_plugin/tests/watcher_liveness_gate_smoke.py

# WS-2e §4.3.3a (Day's live route-theft find) — peer_register refuses to bind
# under a session id a DIFFERENT LIVE session already holds. Without it, a
# watcher launched from another session's shell inherits its AGENT_SESSION_ID
# and the register self-refresh silently re-points EVERY role that session
# holds. R7b is the safety half and the reason the gate is LIVENESS-gated
# rather than existence-gated: a restart always brings a new instance id under
# the same session id, so an existence gate would refuse every legitimate
# succession — worse than the theft it prevents, and R7a alone cannot see it.
plugins/agent_messaging_plugin/tests/register_session_conflict_smoke.py

# iss_0cc6f884 (measured 2026-09-05) — the sibling defect R7 could not see. R7
# guards the LIVE-incumbent case; this guards the DEAD-incumbent case R7
# deliberately waves through as subprocess succession. A Claude Code pre-warmed
# spare inherited a week-dead session's AGENT_SESSION_ID from the daemon's
# environment and was handed that session's durable coordination role by
# refresh_role_binding_cas — which re-points every binding filtered on
# agent_session_id ALONE — without ever calling peer_claim_role. The
# discriminator is parent_pid: a bridge resuming its own session keeps its host
# process, an inheritor brings its own. The safety cases matter as much as the
# killing one: an over-broad rule here would silently strip roles from every
# bridge reconnect (verified by mutation — the two directions fail disjoint
# cases).
plugins/agent_messaging_plugin/tests/register_orphan_session_id_smoke.py

# Cron UTC-compliance repair (2026-08-16 lane-utc-scheduler). SchedulerManager's
# BackgroundScheduler is configured `timezone=datetime.UTC`, but `add_cron_job`
# called `CronTrigger.from_crontab(cron_expression)` with no `timezone` kwarg --
# APScheduler's CronTrigger.__init__ resolves an unset timezone to
# get_localzone() (the HOST's local tz) at construction time, never consulting
# the scheduler's own UTC config, despite from_crontab's docstring claiming it
# defaults to the scheduler timezone. Measured: on the deploy host,
# CronTrigger.from_crontab('30 7 * * *').timezone resolved to America/Vancouver,
# a -25200s offset -- the same magnitude independently measured in the
# peer_list.updated_at UTC-naive bug. The plugin's own KB doc claims "All cron
# expressions use UTC timezone", making the doc part of the defect. Pins a
# fixed non-UTC get_localzone() (not the host's real zone) so the assertion
# stays meaningful on a UTC-local CI host. Covers both the live add_cron_job
# call site and the restore_schedules path (same call site, delegated).
# Hermetic -- real SchedulerManager/APScheduler, get_localzone patched, no DB.
plugins/default_scheduling_plugin/tests/cron_utc_timezone_smoke.py

# ActionExecutor-not-initialized deferred-fire repair (2026-08-16
# lane-utc-scheduler, defect A). Reproduced from
# profile/data/logs/2026-08-15_profile.log: sch-2n0h8hh6jp9te and three other
# schedules fired during two separate blue-green swap startup windows, before
# set_action_factory had run in the candidate process. The old code's
# _mark_schedule_failed on this path persisted schedules.status=error, which
# SchedulerManager.restore_schedules reads to SKIP the schedule on the very
# next restart -- a one-shot write, not an ongoing firing loop, is what killed
# sch-2n0h8hh6jp9te permanently. Pins BOTH halves of the fix: the fire-time
# write must leave schedules.status untouched (universal job tracker gets
# 'pending', an allowed asynchronous_jobs.status CHECK value, not 'failed'),
# AND -- per explicit rider, since a shallower smoke could miss a regression
# that only re-breaks restore -- the resulting schedule must actually SURVIVE
# SchedulerManager.restore_schedules and get re-armed in APScheduler, not just
# avoid the status write in isolation.
# Hermetic -- real SchedulingPlugin/SchedulerManager, fake repository/tracker
# doubles, no DB.
plugins/default_scheduling_plugin/tests/action_executor_deferred_fire_smoke.py

# Result-processing contracts (c) — a plugin's TYPED ErrorDetail must survive to
# the stored action result. `_mark_action_failed` stored a constant
# code="action_failed" on every failure, so every /process/call consumer
# platform-wide saw one code and could not discriminate failure classes. The
# constant was the symptom: the signature took `error_message: str` and all
# three call sites stringified first — one of them threw away a full ErrorDetail
# dict it already held. Drives the REAL _mark_action_failed (only the DB seams
# and the downstream dispatch hop are stubbed) and asserts on what is STORED.
# Carries the untyped control: no typing in => the historical constant out, so
# the fix cannot be satisfied by inventing codes it does not have.
ananta/tests/core/actions/action_failure_error_typing_smoke.py

# completion-wake routing (2026-08-14, lane-w-completion-wake) -- the PUSH half
# of the background-job completion contract, whose durable/retrievable half the
# job_service smokes above cover. The core smoke pins the replace decision: a
# bridge-origin flow carrying a completion_route_role stamp submits the delivery
# verb INSTEAD of the inference continuation, an unstamped flow stays pull-only
# (the negative control that keeps coverage honestly partial), errors route like

# verb's sender ladder would resolve a bridge-origin completion's sender to the
# job's own originator, stamping every completion as sent by its recipient.

# oversized action payloads + action-path liveness (2026-08-15,
# lane-ac-payload-guard) -- the "cannot happen again" half of the 2026-08-15
# action-queue freeze, whose root cause was an 82 MB deliver_result payload
# whose json.loads held the GIL for two hours and starved every other thread.
# The smoke's load-bearing case is [3]: it hands the claim guard input that is
# BOTH over the bound and unparseable, so a guard placed downstream of
# json.loads raises a decode error while a correct pre-parse guard raises the
# size refusal -- the only assertion that can distinguish a guard which fires in
# time from one that has already caused the outage it was meant to prevent. The
# fixtures ADVANCE (a real over-bound string, a real over-cap record set) rather
# than asserting on constants, and every guard here was red-mutated: disabling
# the enqueue check, parsing before measuring, restoring the unbounded read,
# restoring the queue-depth gate on the liveness alarm (GAU-10, 2026-08-18 --
# the gate suppressed the alarm forever on a freeze that began at depth 0,
# since only the poller being killed could ever unstick it), and requeueing an
# oversized orphan each turn exactly the check that names that property red.
# Offline by construction: pure functions and a spy, no solet, no database.
ananta/tests/core/actions/oversized_payload_guard_smoke.py

# adopter issue #9 -- the split result envelope (2026-08-15,
# lane-ae-issue9-envelope). An envelope came back carrying a success payload
# AND an unsupported_on_host error. Both halves were real: error_message is one
# mutable column on the action_events row, result is the latest of many
# action_results rows, and one action row that executed twice ends up pairing
# attempt 1's error with attempt 2's success. The completed-status writer never
# cleared the column; its failure counterpart always sets it.
#
# The smoke registers BOTH halves of the fix, because the first alone is a
# regression: a bare clear makes the envelope clean and the double execution
# INVISIBLE, deleting the only surviving evidence that an action row ever runs
# twice. So the clear must announce itself -- a WARNING carrying the action_id
# and both timestamps, plus a counter on get_metrics().
#
# Red-mutated four ways, each flipping a different named check: dropping the
# clear reproduces the adopter's exact envelope; clearing SILENTLY (the trap --
# the one-line patch) leaves the envelope check green and reddens all seven
# instrument checks; announcing unconditionally reddens the clean-completion
# control; and swallowing an unreadable pre-clear read reddens the blind-spot
# check. Nothing under test is stubbed -- the two shipped status writers, the
# shipped result store and the shipped envelope reader all run against an
# envelope-faithful in-memory pair of tables. Offline by construction: no
# solet, no database, no model turn.
ananta/tests/core/actions/completion_clears_stale_error_smoke.py

# query_state_bounds_smoke (2026-08-15, lane B of the unbounded-state-read
# programme): pins the MAX_READ_ROWS row bound at EVERY query_state
# implementation, which had four and they disagreed.
#
# Registered mainly as a REGRESSION PIN ON A FALSE PREMISE. The programme's
# census recorded 70 query_state call sites as structurally unable to bound
# themselves, on the reasoning that its (namespace, filters) signature has no
# limit slot. It has one: the filters dict IS read_state's query dict, so
# limit/unbounded pass straight through and always did. Section 1 measures that
# end-to-end so the belief cannot be re-derived from the signature and acted on
# a second time -- the census instrument itself encoded it and mis-filed a call
# site that was already passing limit=1.
#
# The two REAL holes it closes, neither of which the census could see because it
# classified call sites and never implementations: BootstrapDatabaseStorage
# carried its own copy of the body that skipped the cap its sibling read_state
# applies to the same rows off the same in-memory store; and the transaction
# query_state had no bound at all -- which mattered because StateTransaction
# exposes no read_state, making it the ONLY row-returning read primitive inside
# a transaction, with no bounded alternative to migrate to.
#
# Red-mutated in both directions per hole, each reddening a different named
# check: undoing the bootstrap delegation reddens the over-cap refusal; making
# bootstrap read_state actually filter (the opposite direction, and the change
# deliberately NOT made) reddens the no-result-change checks; dropping the txn
# overflow raise reddens the never-truncate pair; and dropping the fetch_limit
# pass-through reddens the LIMIT-compilation pair. Section 4 is the load-bearing
# one: an over-cap unbounded read must RAISE, never return a prefix.
#
# PURE UNIT, offline by construction: real implementations against recording
# fakes. No solet, no database, no model turn.
ananta/tests/services/state_service/query_state_bounds_smoke.py

# --- Lane A, unbounded state reads (2026-08-15 SCAN_UNBOUNDED remediation) ---
#
# bounded_read_ceiling_smoke pins the CALL-SITE half of the read-bound contract.
# read_bounds owns the provider cap; bounded_read owns what a site must do when
# it passes an explicit limit to read a whole small table. The asymmetry it
# guards: no limit -> loud refusal past the cap, explicit limit ->
# overflow_is_error=False -> SILENT PREFIX past the limit. So "just add a limit"
# converts a site that failed loudly at 10,000 into one that quietly returns 500
# of 600. The boundary is >= (at exactly `ceiling` rows a result cannot be told
# from a truncated one); the M1 calibration mutates precisely that.
#
# unbounded_scan_repair_behaviour_smoke asserts the repaired sites DO THEIR JOB
# rather than merely pass a limit. Each of the three carried two defects — an
# unbounded read AND a result navigated through a data["result"] key that
# read_state has never returned — so a bound-only assertion would have gone green
# against all three at once. Calibrated with the navigation reverted while the
# bound is KEPT (M4): the resolved-value checks redden while every filter/limit
# check stays green.
#
# PURE UNIT, offline by construction: real production classes against recording
# spy state services. No solet, no database, no model turn.
ananta/tests/services/state_service/bounded_read_ceiling_smoke.py
ananta/tests/services/state_service/unbounded_scan_repair_behaviour_smoke.py

# 2026-08-15 read-cap boot repair — the three agent_messaging_plugin startup
# backfills, all of which read a whole table with no limit on the BOOT path.
# _run_startup_backfills runs them before the FastAPI surface comes up, so a
# refused read there does not degrade a feature, it stops the bridge starting:
# that is exactly what happened when the default read bound dropped to 100 and
# role_class_backfill's 133-row role_binding read was refused, taking agent
# messaging and the action queue down with it.
#
# All three reads are paginated now (bounded_read.iter_table_rows). Each smoke
# carries a fixture that STRADDLES THREE PAGES, because the property under test
# is completeness across a page boundary and a fixture below the boundary cannot
# test it — the pre-repair role_class smoke was green on five rows throughout the
# outage. Each also wraps RealShapeState in CapEnforcingState, which refuses an
# over-bound unbounded read the way the provider does; without it these smokes
# stay green against code that cannot boot at all.
#
# role_class_backfill_smoke plants its cardinality violation on page THREE (a
# single-page read reports a clean fleet) and is red-first proven against the
# pre-fix source: reverting the read reproduces the live
# query.unbounded_read_over_cap refusal.
#
# message_important_backfill_smoke EXISTED BUT WAS NEVER REGISTERED HERE, so it
# had never run. role_message_consumed_backfill_smoke is new — that module's
# docstring claimed "S7 exercises it against a real state fake" and no such test
# existed anywhere in the tree.
#
# PURE UNIT, offline: in-memory state over real ActionResult shapes, with pages
# served by the real ordered-query primitives. No solet, no database.
plugins/agent_messaging_plugin/tests/message_important_backfill_smoke.py
plugins/agent_messaging_plugin/tests/role_message_consumed_backfill_smoke.py

# read-cap sibling sweep, 2026-08-15 PDT / 2026-08-16 UTC (lane-ak). Pins
# read.py's per-session event walk after it stopped being an unbounded
# query_state + Python sort over a 2,077,082-row table.
#
# REGISTERED IN THE SAME CHANGE AS THE CODE, deliberately. The sibling
# read_migration_live_smoke.py is NOT in this register and therefore has never
# run in the suite — a 360/365 green says nothing about a file outside the
# allowlist. That gap is exactly what this sweep found when it changed that
# smoke and then could not demonstrate the change was exercised.
#
# The fixture is DELIBERATELY UNLIKE PRODUCTION and must stay that way: live
# data is dense with zero created_at ties, i.e. benign in both dimensions a
# cursor can get wrong, so a production-shaped fixture would test nothing. Its
# created_at-tie group is the only executable evidence for the "wrong tool"
# rule in bounded_read.iter_table_rows' docstring; delete it and "simplifying"
# this site onto the shared helper starts passing review.
#
# Red-first calibrated against 4 mutations: dropped id tie-break (4 red),
# first-page-only (5 red), full-page-means-done (5 red), and ordering by
# created_at instead of sequence (1 red — precisely the ordering assertion).
#
# PURE UNIT, offline: a query_ordered fake that honours row-value `after`
# cursors, refuses a cursor/order_by arity mismatch, and applies the
# include_deleted default. No solet, no database.
ananta/tests/llm/session_ledger/event_sequence_walk_smoke.py

# 2026-08-16 read-cap, second round — the flow-scoped counters in core/.
# Both sites read every matching row and returned len(rows), and both were
# found by the census and classified HOT, then parked in a tier-2 list whose
# predicates were SAMPLED by eye rather than measured. {flow_id_trace, state}
# and {flow_id_trace, status} read like narrow keys; they matched 129 and 2,902.
#
# FlowRuntimeGraph.get_pending_token_count is reached from complete_token on
# EVERY action completion — it failed loudly and broke the completion path.
# ActionQueuePoller._get_flow_error_count is worse because it is QUIET: its
# record extractor graceful-degrades a refused read to [], so it reported 0
# errors for a flow with 2,902 and the max-flow-errors CIRCUIT BREAKER (threshold
# 3) silently stopped firing — on a path only ever taken because a flow is
# already going wrong.
#
# Both repaired with the scalar `count` verb rather than a bound: the callers
# wanted a number, and count runs the aggregate in the owner plugin and ships no
# rows, so it is outside the row cap entirely. get_pending_tokens genuinely needs
# its rows and is paginated instead.
#
# Red-first proven: reverting the FRG count reproduces the live production error
# string verbatim ("Failed to query pending token count for flow ..."); reverting
# the poller count reddens 3 checks; degrading the scalar extractor to 0 on a
# non-completed envelope reddens 2. That last mutation was GREEN until
# calibration found the branch uncovered — restoring 0 there would have
# re-silenced the breaker one layer down without failing anything.
#
# PURE UNIT, offline: pages served by the real ordered-query primitives, and the
# fake REFUSES an over-cap unbounded read the way the provider does. Without that
# refusal the smoke would pass against code that cannot run in production.
ananta/tests/core/state/flow_counter_bound_smoke.py

# session_page_walk_smoke — pins read_support.walk_sessions_page, which replaced
# list_sessions' unbounded whole-table read (14,412 rows to return 50; MEASURED
# DEAD on rel-20260816T042114Z-41eed531f with cap_rows=100).
#
# Its fixture is DELIBERATELY UNLIKE PRODUCTION and must stay that way. The
# defining property is POSITION, not size: qualifying rows lie BEYOND the first
# page ("band A" = 90 non-qualifying rows at the head of the sort order), because
# a big fixture whose survivors all sit in page 1 exercises the cursor exactly as
# little as a 5-row one. Band A is also the ONLY reason the inlined M3 check —
# pushing `limit` down without post-filtering, which silently UNDER-RETURNS — is
# detectable at all. If M3 ever passes trivially, band A has been weakened:
# repair the fixture, do not delete the test.
#
# Red-first calibrated: dropped id tie-break (RED: 10 of 20 tie-group rows lost
# at a page boundary), non-advancing cursor (RED: walk ceiling raises), dropped
# pushed-down equality filters (RED: wrong-vendor row appears). The tie-break
# mutation did NOT redden on the first attempt — the fixture contained the tie
# group but no test reached it at the default limit; that gap was found by
# running the mutation and is now closed by test_tie_group_survives_a_page_boundary.
#
# list_sessions_m17_filters_smoke — EXISTED SINCE M17 AND HAD NEVER BEEN
# REGISTERED, so none of its 26 checks had ever run in the suite. Third
# unregistered smoke found during this sweep. It covers list_sessions' windows,
# order variants, limit, canonical-only default, vendor filtering and both
# source_kind routes — i.e. exactly the surface wave 2a changes.
#

ananta/tests/llm/session_ledger/session_page_walk_smoke.py

# read-cap sweep census follow-on, 2026-08-16 (lane-ak).
#
# census_fold_smoke — EXISTED SINCE THE 2026-06-20 CENSUS MIGRATION AND HAD NEVER
# BEEN REGISTERED, so none of its checks had ever run in the suite. FOURTH
# unregistered smoke found during this sweep (after message_important_backfill,
# core_slice3_migration_live, and list_sessions_m17_filters). It is the ONLY
# behavioural coverage of build_census, including the change registered here.
#
# It now also pins the streaming property: build_census reads __source whole (21
# rows) but STREAMS session / tool_call / import_batch through base.walk_table
# rather than reading them whole (27,208 / 637,496 / 284,787 rows measured;
# `census` was DEAD on rel-20260816T042114Z-41eed531f, refusing on `session`).
#
# Its `walk` fake is a GENERATOR on purpose. Streaming exposed a trap that lists
# had hidden: _CensusAggregator consumed `sessions` TWICE — once to index, once
# to tally — which is merely wasteful for a list and silently WRONG for a
# generator (the second pass sees an exhausted iterator and every session tally
# comes back empty, with no error). A list-returning fake would let that bug pass.
#
# Red-first calibrated: reintroducing the double consumption (RED, 4 checks incl.
# session_count=0), and dropping the id->source index build (RED, 5 checks — the
# tool_call and event joins lose their session lookup).
#
# PURE UNIT, offline: in-memory reader + state shim over real ActionResult shapes.

# read-cap sweep wave 2b, 2026-08-16 (lane-ak).
#
# session_kind_membership_smoke — pins list_sessions(source_kind=...), which was
# over the 100-row cap TWICE: _junction_canonical_ids read __session_source_kind
# whole (4,005 rows for claude_code_local, 8,055 total, MEASURED), and its output
# then became `id = ANY(those 4,005)` on __session. The live refusal on the
# serving release named session_source_kind — the FIRST of the two — which is why
# the junction was repaired first.
#
# THE FAKE ENFORCES THE CAP, and that is the whole mechanism: it raises when an
# `= ANY` filter carries more than MAX_READ_ROWS values, and when an unlimited
# read returns more than MAX_READ_ROWS rows. Against a permissive fake an
# unchunked read succeeds quietly and the smoke certifies the bug it exists to
# catch. The refusal IS the test.
#
# That fake earned itself immediately: it caught a real defect in the first
# version of the repair. base._query_membership_chunked claims "a chunk matches
# at most its own length" — true only for a UNIQUE column. external_session_id is
# non-unique BY DESIGN (a canonical and its siblings share it), so a 100-value
# chunk returned 200 rows and was refused. That read is now chunked AND PAGED.
#
# Fixture size (120) is bounded on BOTH sides: above MAX_READ_ROWS so the walk
# pages and the read chunks, and below list_sessions' own limit clamp of 200 so a
# complete result is not truncated by the clamp. The first attempt used 250 and
# the verb correctly returned 200 — indistinguishable from a lost chunk.
#
# PURE UNIT, offline: cap-enforcing in-memory state over real ActionResult shapes.
ananta/tests/llm/session_ledger/session_kind_membership_smoke.py

# read-cap sweep, managed_session fleet list, 2026-08-16 (lane-ak).
#
# managed_session_list_bound_smoke — pins list_managed_sessions, the ONE fleet
# list behind plugin::agent_messaging_plugin::list_sessions. Its no-filter call
# was MEASURED REFUSING on the serving release: query.unbounded_read_over_cap,
# table managed_session, cap_rows 100, against 106 live rows. The ledger is
# append-mostly and unpruned, so it crossed the bound by accumulating history —
# a bound that fails as the fleet gets MORE use, same shape as the RUNNING
# import-batch set repaired earlier in this sweep.
#
# Fixture is 250 rows at a 100-row page so the walk must page more than once;
# the live table was 106, a near-miss, which is exactly why the refusal was a
# surprise. It also pins the SOFT-DELETE OVERRIDE: the old code seeded
# {is_deleted: 0} and let a caller's filters overwrite it, so is_deleted=1
# returned soft-deleted rows. iter_table_rows expresses that as include_deleted,
# so the request is TRANSLATED, not dropped — no in-repo caller uses it, but
# list_sessions forwards arbitrary filters, so removing it silently would be a
# behaviour change smuggled inside a bound fix.
#
# The fake ENFORCES the cap. Red-first calibrated: reverting to the pre-fix
# unbounded read reproduces the LIVE production error verbatim
# ("'managed_session' returned 250 rows against the 100-row cap"); a page-1-only
# walk returns 100 of 250; dropping the override translation returns 250 instead
# of 30 for is_deleted=1.
#
# PURE UNIT, offline: cap-enforcing in-memory state over real ActionResult shapes.
plugins/agent_messaging_plugin/tests/managed_session_list_bound_smoke.py

# F6/F7 gate repair (2026-08-16, lane-ai) — two defects in the gates themselves.
#
# 1. A gate that CRASHED was rendered as a gate that found VIOLATIONS: the
#    aggregate mapped every unrecognised exit code to "BLOCKING", so radon_cc's
#    RecursionError printed as "Blocking gate violations (radon_cc)" — complexity
#    debt in the reader's own code, asserted about code nothing had measured.
# 2. The scope walks were filesystem-based and reached vendored trees
#    (18,321 .py under plugins/cosyvoice2_tts_plugin/src/.venv_cosyvoice).
#
# Pins the scope predicate from BOTH sides, because the first fix over-corrected
# to tracked-only and produced a FALSE GREEN inside the hour — a brand-new
# never-added smoke with a CC-12 went unmeasured while the aggregate reported
# clean. So: a brand-new untracked file MUST be gated; a gitignored vendored
# tree must NOT be. Fixing either direction alone is how this went wrong twice.
#
# Every assertion is mutation-calibrated: reverting the wrappers to rglob, the
# scope to tracked-only, the classifier to "unknown exit = violation", or the
# exit-code-before-traceback ordering each turns this smoke red, and the
# negative control (a real violation still reads as a violation) stays green.
#
# PURE UNIT, offline: synthetic CompletedProcess objects + a tempdir; no
# subprocess, no gate run, no timing dependence.
quality_gates/tests/gate_crash_rendering_smoke.py

# Context-gauge storage layer (2026-08-16, lane-ai) — the verbs behind the
# rotation policy had NO plugin-side test at all before this. Their entire
# coverage was two hook-side fakes scripting the response envelope
# ({"status":"completed","result":{"data":{}}}), which assert nothing about
# contents and therefore CANNOT go red when the interface widens — they just
# keep passing. Widening safely required creating coverage, not updating it.
#
# Pins the tri-state contract that widening threatens: cache fields are
# true / false / NOT REPORTED, and a reporter that omits them must never read
# back as "cache is warm". Mutation-calibrated — coercing None to False in the
# store, coercing it in the read-back, or dropping the cache view entirely
# (the pre-widening shape) each turn this smoke red.
#
# Also pins that rotation_band is DERIVED at read time from the live policy
# constants rather than stored, so a threshold change never needs a backfill.
#
# PURE UNIT, offline: a recording state double, no database, no bridge.
plugins/agent_messaging_plugin/tests/context_status_verbs_smoke.py

# release-pointer stability (2026-08-17, lane-release-pointer) — the spawn-env
# half of deploy survival. Every spawn surface pinned the solet CLI into a
# VERSIONED release dir (AGENT_WAKE_CLI, the PATH prepend, and the long-lived
# `solet watch` registration sidecar's argv[0]), and a deploy REAPS old
# releases, so one cutover dangled every long-running worker's CLI at once —
# silently, because each consumer is non-fatal by design. Measured twice
# (lane-ai dark both ways across two deploys, 2026-08-16).
#
# Pins that the resolved path is expressed through the atomically-swapped
# `current` pointer, and — the leg that matters — that the pointer lands
# UNRESOLVED: a rewrite that calls Path.resolve() still names a real file
# TODAY, so every ordinary liveness check stays green while the version pin
# survives untouched. The suite asserts the release id is textually ABSENT
# from what reaches the environment, which is the only form that mutation
# cannot pass.
#
# Includes a DEPLOY-SWAP SIMULATION leg (old release dir removed, pointer
# moved) that re-stats AND re-executes the worker's pre-deploy env, plus a
# negative control proving the simulation really reaped something. Also pins
# the two refusals — mid-cutover version skew, and a pointer whose target was
# reaped — and the Repair-4 non-regression that a dangling pointer never
# breaks a session that would otherwise resolve solet from PATH, and never
# raises.
#
# Mutation-calibrated, 5 named mutations each verified RED: no-op rewrite;
# Path.resolve() re-pin; skew check dropped; stat/exec guard dropped;
# stabilization applied at expose_worker_cli only (which leaves the sidecar
# and worker_path pinned, i.e. registration still dies on deploy).
#
# PURE UNIT, offline: a synthetic release layout in a temp dir with real
# symlinks and executable stubs. No tmux server, bridge, database, network,
# deploy, or model turn.
plugins/agent_messaging_plugin/tests/release_pointer_stability_smoke.py

# GAU-21 (2026-08-19) DURABLE GAUGE-NOTICE RECORD — a gauge alarm now leaves a
# trace, INCLUDING the alarm that reached nobody. The sweep's notices were
# in-memory bridge events only: a restart lost them, the only reader REMOVED
# what it read (so a verifier polling that queue could swallow the steward's
# own notice), nothing was keyed on type, and the notify path returned early on
# an unbound steward — so an alarm that fired into the void and one that never
# fired were the same silence.
#
# Everything is driven through the REAL sweep legs rather than by calling the
# store to make a row appear, because the wiring is where the original defect
# lived. Covers: the record survives a delivery that never happened
# (no_steward_binding as a recorded outcome, not an early return); the three
# delivery outcomes stay distinct rather than collapsing to a boolean; a record
# fault never costs another row its notice; retention is a HARD bound scoped
# per (subject, type); reading does not consume; an omitted filter does not
# narrow; and release attribution is read or honestly absent, never a phantom.
#
# PURE UNIT, offline: real-shape state fake plus a real in-memory bridge. No
# database, network, or model turn.
plugins/agent_messaging_plugin/tests/gauge_notice_record_smoke.py

# write while the lifecycle clock keeps advancing (so it reproduces the real
# freeze signature instead of an idle session, which the detector correctly

# Public acts (2026-09-19, chg_61d13a17, design §5.4 stages 9-12): seed release
# tag / manager GitHub release / tap Formula commit / post-publish read-back,
# driven through the public API against a FAKE gh + REAL local git over file://
# bare repos. The fake CommandRunner refuses any git argv naming a network host
# and answers gh from an in-memory releases API, so no run can touch GitHub or a
# real tap. Covers both concern-#1 world states (release exists + asset absent =
# resumed; asset sha differs = release_conflict), release notes picked by label,
# the tap diff-stat/parent/identity read-back, a real non-fast-forward tap push,
# and the never-delete / never-force invariant over the module's own argv log.
# publish_release stage machine (2026-09-19, chg_5cce07da, design §5): the ONE
# orchestrating release verb, driven through all 14 stages against a FAKE
# world + FAKE state service (no network, gh, Tart, assemble or seal). Covers
# both checkpoint shapes (§5.6), dry_run, re-issue (§7.5), the append-path
# re-stage (pushed sha != sealed sha), every named refusal per stage with the
# lock released and the public world left as found, idempotent resume (kill
# mid-stage-10 → 'resumed'; a completed run re-entered → every public act
# 'unchanged'), the orphan sweep / cooperative stop / in-stage crash, the
# release_runs + release_locks schema and store discipline. The born-async job
# adapter (dispatch → queued row → worker → status on the job, the orphan
# sweep on first poll) is its sibling smoke. Both share tests/_release_fakes.py.

# reproduce_release (2026-09-19, chg_505884b2, design §7.5(d)): customer
# report -> exact local rebuild. Resolution (release_tag XOR attestation,
# an unknown release, a pre-manifest release with no manifest asset all
# refuse), the live-world independent read-back (reused from
# public_acts.verify_published -- a tampered tap propagates
# post_publish_verification_failed), the rebuilt payload/Formula/lock/
# profile-lock hash comparison (a genuine mismatch raises
# ReproductionMismatchError naming the class), and guest-validation gating
# (not requested; requested with no guest_validation to replay; requested
# against a manifest that names one -> guest_ladder_unavailable, the driver
# not being built yet). Stage 5 itself is a fake subprocess seam --
# stage_release.py has its own dedicated smoke above -- so this covers only
# reproduce_release.py's own resolve/verify/compare logic.
# not registered: plugins/seed_factory_plugin/tests/_release_fixtures.py
# shared smoke support module (FakeGitHub/FakeRunner/FakeStageRunner).

# reissue (2026-09-19, chg_505884b2, design §5.4, §7.5(d)):
# publish_release(reissue_of=<tag>) support, standalone against the
# documented stage contract (release_run.py, chg_5cce07da, does not exist
# in this checkout yet -- this module is the seam the sibling lane calls
# once it lands). Resolution by an existing release_manifests row (reuses
# its evidence) vs. a bare pre-manifest seed tag + live PROVENANCE (forces
# a fresh full gate -- SignatureEvidenceRequiredError refuses a pre-manifest
# reissue with no fresh_gate_verdict supplied, never inferring historical
# signatures); a caller-supplied seed_repository/profile/channel_id/
# manager_source_repository that disagrees with a resolved manifest's own
# recorded values refuses ReissueParameterMismatchError; an unresolvable
# reissue_of refuses ReissueTargetUnknownError. Stages 8 (publish_seed) and
# 9 (tag_published_seed) are proven to ACTUALLY short-circuit to
# "unchanged" against the seed's own already-published repo (not merely
# asserted), stage 11 runs before stage 10 so the uploaded manifest carries
# a complete tap section, stage 12 (verify_published) passes against the
# real fixture world, and the FINAL manifest round-trips through
# release_manifest.set_release_manifest/get_release_manifest.

# Completion-set freeze and unknown-reads-as-false (rollup-cluster 1.41,
# 2026-09-02): a completion probe gated on a reachable-but-unanswered decision
# must stay required rather than read as not-required, a genuinely unreachable
# gate must not block convergence, and the set must RE-SCOPE on answer revision
# instead of freezing at create. Both defects fail GREEN (an omitted completion
# probe lets the roll-up report VERIFIED), so the load-bearing assertion is that
# the re-admitted probe actually RUNS and records an attempt -- membership alone
# is not a discriminator. A probe that already ran is retained through a prune:
# _expected_attempt_stage resolves completion attempts through that map, so
# dropping one would fail journal validation. Offline: shipped flow JSON, temp
# dirs, stubbed adapter.
solet_cli/tests/completion_set_rebind_smoke.py

# Retained attempts of unbound operations are history, not corruption
# (rollup-cluster 1.56, iss_f103ec49, 2026-09-02): journal validation resolved an
# attempt's expected stage through the CURRENT bindings, so a legitimately
# re-scoped plan orphaned its own history and the journal failed to load. Pins
# BOTH branches -- an absent operation is tolerated, a still-bound operation
# whose stage disagrees still fails -- and asserts on the specific error, since
# other validators reject the same document for their own reasons. Also pins the
# leak direction: a pruned operation's retained attempt must not feed
# stage-status derivation. Offline: shipped flow JSON only.
solet_cli/tests/retained_attempt_validation_smoke.py

# Reconciliation must not carry retired operations forward (rollup-cluster 1.42,
# 2026-09-02): an operation the SOURCE revision declared but the DESTINATION does
# not kept feeding the destination stage roll-up, pinning a stage that nothing
# could clear. Driven through the real reconcile_contract_stage_probe_state path.
# Depends on 1.56 by construction -- the retained attempt of the dropped
# operation only survives validation with that fix in place, so a revert of 1.56
# fails here loudly. Offline: shipped flow JSON only.
solet_cli/tests/reconciliation_operation_scope_smoke.py

# Genesis marker pair comparator (D-8.F2 iss_3de66b3c, D-8-LATENT iss_c010206a,
# 2026-09-05): a genesis run writes its history twice -- _finalize_marker writes
# the RICHER profile/data/github_midwife/attempt.json from steps+phases, and
# _write_genesis_marker writes the POORER .solet/genesis.json from the spine
# alone. The probe and the installation doctor both key on the poorer record and
# nothing compared the two. The divergence has teeth because post-spine phase
# failures are caught and appended rather than raised, and _finalize_marker
# ("success") then runs unconditionally: a target whose install_router failed
# carries an attempt record saying success WITH a failed phase, and a completion
# marker that never mentions it. This registers the missing comparator. It does
# NOT claim to compare a target marker against the manager's CURRENT genesis step
# list -- that list lives in the midwife plugin, which the manager never imports
# (it reaches it only by path into the target's own checkout), so iss_c010206a is
# covered as the recorded-spine baseline the artifact itself lacks, not as a
# fired manager-vs-target skew. Offline: constructed marker files under a
# temporary directory only.
solet_cli/tests/doctor_genesis_marker_census_smoke.py

# Postgres major pin comparator (D-E1 iss_9d6b61ba, 2026-09-05): layer-0 repair
# is UNPINNED while its acceptance is PINNED -- bootstrap.ensure_postgres reaches
# its ABSENT branch and runs `brew install postgresql` unversioned, then returns
# installed_and_started WITHOUT re-probing the major it installed, while both
# layers require _SUPPORTED_POSTGRES_MAJOR. The sibling ensure_pgvector does
# re-probe and raises on disagreement, and probe_postgres already owns a
# RUNNING_WRONG_VERSION state it only ever applies to a PRE-EXISTING install.
# This registers the comparison at doctor time. It does NOT claim to detect the
# missing re-probe itself -- that is installer control flow, and source scanning
# is the wrong instrument for a behavioural property; what is measured is the
# consequence, the running major versus the pinned major. Note for the shipped
# smoke filter: besides its own module this smoke reads the repo-root
# bootstrap.py, to pin one integer literal against the census's copy so the two
# cannot drift silently. Offline: constructed `psql --version` output only.
solet_cli/tests/doctor_postgres_pin_census_smoke.py

# Router identity read-back (D-7.4 iss_c30be917, 2026-09-05): _verify_router_up
# returns on ANY status reply containing router_started_at -- no pid, port or
# identity comparison -- so an INCUMBENT router already listening at the socket is
# accepted as the install's own: install reports success over a foreign router,
# publishes an unused port, and the new launchd job crash-loops under KeepAlive.
# router_mgmt._reclaim_socket_path refuses correctly inside the incoming router,
# but the installer never consumes that verdict. The comparison was always
# available and never made: InstanceRecord has carried expected_router_socket and
# expected_router_port_range since create time, and a whole-tree read finds them
# written, serialised and listed -- compared against a live router NOWHERE. This
# registers the read-back, discriminating on the published colour port, which is
# the same predicate the row's own fix names. It does NOT compare pid or the
# router's bound management port: the status payload carries neither today, and
# adding them is the install-time fix, not this check. Offline: constructed status
# payloads only; no socket is opened.
solet_cli/tests/doctor_router_identity_census_smoke.py

# Offline credential copy currency (D-2.F2 iss_dd451098, 2026-09-05):
# credential_seed.seed_db_password rotates the live role and exactly TWO Keychain
# copies (state db_password, pgvector password) and never the THIRD -- the legacy
# raw `password` account that default_knowledge_plugin/tools/_pg_credentials.py
# reads, which CLAUDE.md prescribes as the degraded fallback when live retrieval
# is down. That file's docstring states the contract rotation breaks: "Both
# accounts hold the same password", so the break surfaces only when the fallback
# is already being reached for. The predicate is deliberately NOT "the live copy
# is newer": db_password was introduced additively by G3 AFTER the raw account, so
# newer is the normal healthy state (measured: live 20260628, offline 20260623,
# both unmodified since creation) and a naive check warns on every solet. The warn
# requires a ROTATION (mdat > cdat) postdating the offline write. No secret VALUE
# is ever read, compared or emitted -- attribute reads omit -w, which also avoids
# the SecurityAgent ACL prompt that would hang a headless run; the stated cost is
# that equal timestamps do not PROVE equal values. Offline: constructed attribute
# mappings only; `security` is never executed.
solet_cli/tests/doctor_credential_copy_census_smoke.py
solet_cli/tests/doctor_terminal_return_keys_smoke.py

# Plugin version source agreement, FORK-NEUTRAL (D-8-u4 iss_1e3a1558,
# 2026-09-05): plugin_install converges to the wrong fixed point -- probe success
# is visible AND interpreter_ok, visibility checks only id/installed/enabled, and
# the module contains ZERO version reads, so a stale-but-enabled plugin is a
# STABLE verified state and the correctly-wired remediation hangs off a sensor
# that cannot sense the condition. The row records that no version-aware probe
# exists anywhere, measured. This is that probe, and it is deliberately neutral on
# the row's OPEN fix fork (authoritative source = packaged plugin.json vs
# marketplace manifest, which may disagree): it compares three version-bearing
# sources -- the registry, the installed cache copy that actually executes, and
# the packaged manifest located via the marketplace source pointer -- warns ONLY
# on disagreement, declares none authoritative, and prescribes NO repair. The
# smoke asserts that neutrality directly, not just the detection. Verified against
# live machine state while written: registry 0.6.0 / cache 0.6.0 / packaged 0.8.1,
# the reg-58 incident, undetected until now. Offline: constructed manifest trees
# under a temporary directory only.
solet_cli/tests/doctor_plugin_version_skew_census_smoke.py
